  5025: void VulkanCommandProcessor::FinalizeBdNativeColorMirrorAfterPass() {
  5026:   if (!bd_color_mirror_active_) {
  5027:     return;
  5028:   }
  5029:   // Clear the arm FIRST: MirrorBdNativeColorProducer -> SubmitBarriers ->
  5030:   // EndRenderPass re-enters this function; the false arm makes it a no-op (no
  5031:   // recursion, exactly one mirror per producer pass).
  5032:   bd_color_mirror_active_ = false;
  5033:   const VulkanRenderTargetCache::Framebuffer* fb = bd_color_mirror_fb_;
  5034:   bd_color_mirror_fb_ = nullptr;
  5035:   if (!fb) {
  5036:     return;
  5037:   }
  5038:   // The producer pass is already ended in the recorded stream. The mirror's
  5039:   // barriers + vkCmdCopyImage must not be seen as inside a pass, so temporarily
  5040:   // clear the tracker (the internal SubmitBarriers would otherwise emit a
  5041:   // spurious CmdVkEndRenderPass), then restore it for the caller.
  5042:   VkRenderPass saved_render_pass = current_render_pass_;
  5043:   current_render_pass_ = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
  5044:   render_target_cache_->MirrorBdNativeColorProducer(fb);
  5045:   current_render_pass_ = saved_render_pass;
  5046:   // LEVEL 5: record this finalized native producer as the latest generation, so
  5047:   // the next fullscreen resolve can publish it as the alias for whichever
  5048:   // frontbuffer base it writes. Mirror left the native image SHADER_READ-ready.
  5049:   if (cvars::gpu_bd_native_color_lifetime_hle >= 5 &&
  5050:       fb->bd_native_color_view != VK_NULL_HANDLE &&
  5051:       fb->bd_native_color_lle_rt) {
  5052:     uint32_t src_rt_key = fb->bd_native_color_lle_rt->key().key;
  5053:     BdL5Alias entry;
  5054:     entry.view = fb->bd_native_color_view;
  5055:     entry.fb = fb;
  5056:     entry.generation = ++bd_l5_generation_counter_;
  5057:     entry.frame_epoch = bd_l5_frame_epoch_;
  5058:     entry.width = fb->host_extent.width;
  5059:     entry.height = fb->host_extent.height;
  5060:     entry.src_rt_key = src_rt_key;
  5061:     bd_l5_producer_by_srckey_[src_rt_key] = entry;
  5062:   }
  5063: }

  3374:   framebuffer_entry.fdm_memory = fdm_memory;
  3375:   framebuffer_entry.fdm_view = fdm_view;
  3376:   // Fill the density map now that the framebuffer (which owns the image) exists -
  3377:   // recording the clear+barriers only on the success path avoids a command
  3378:   // referencing an image freed by the failure path above.
  3379:   if (fdm_image != VK_NULL_HANDLE) {
  3380:     FillFragmentDensityMap(fdm_image);
  3381:   }
  3382:   return &framebuffer_entry;
  3383: }
  3384: 
  3385: const VulkanRenderTargetCache::Framebuffer*
  3386: VulkanRenderTargetCache::GetBdNativeColorProducerFramebuffer(
  3387:     RenderPassKey render_pass_key, uint32_t pitch_tiles_at_32bpp,
  3388:     const RenderTarget* const* depth_and_color_render_targets) {
  3389:   // Base LLE framebuffer (created/looked up exactly as the normal path). The
  3390:   // native variant is cached ON this entry, so we reuse its host_extent + key.
  3391:   const Framebuffer* base = GetHostRenderTargetsFramebuffer(
  3392:       render_pass_key, pitch_tiles_at_32bpp, depth_and_color_render_targets);
  3393:   if (!base) {
  3394:     return nullptr;
  3395:   }
  3396:   // Already built for this framebuffer -> reuse.
  3397:   if (base->bd_native_color_framebuffer != VK_NULL_HANDLE) {
  3398:     return base;
  3399:   }
  3400:   // Producer constraints: transfer-format color attachments reinterpret the
  3401:   // image format (ownership-transfer passes) -> the native single-format image
  3402:   // + copy would not match; bail to plain LLE. Require a SINGLE color RT; depth
  3403:   // is OPTIONAL (the pure-LLE field producer has depth 473; the final COMPOSITE
  3404:   // producer is color-only).
  3405:   if (render_pass_key.color_rts_use_transfer_formats) {
  3406:     return base;
  3407:   }
  3408:   uint32_t used = render_pass_key.depth_and_color_used;
  3409:   bool has_depth = (used & (uint32_t(1) << 0)) != 0;
  3410:   uint32_t color_mask = used & ~(uint32_t(1) << 0);
  3411:   uint32_t color_index;
  3412:   if (!xe::bit_scan_forward(color_mask, &color_index)) {
  3413:     return base;  // No color attachment.
  3414:   }
  3415:   if (color_mask & ~(uint32_t(1) << color_index)) {
  3416:     return base;  // More than one color RT -> not the producer shape.
  3417:   }
  3418: 
  3419:   // Cover the WHOLE color chain the EDRAM transfers alias: composite (1280),
  3420:   // field (pitch ~672-720) AND bloom pyramid (320/160/80). Each gets its OWN
  3421:   // native image (no EDRAM aliasing), so once consumers are redirected the
  3422:   // ownership transfers between them are redundant (droppable). >=64 covers
  3423:   // field+bloom; depth-only shadow passes have no color bit so they still bail
  3424:   // above (single-color required). Below 64 = tiny/degenerate, skip.
  3425:   if (base->host_extent.width < 64u) {
  3426:     return base;
  3427:   }
  3428: 
  3429:   const VulkanRenderTarget* depth_rt =
  3430:       has_depth ? static_cast<const VulkanRenderTarget*>(
  3431:                       depth_and_color_render_targets[0])
  3432:                 : nullptr;
  3433:   const auto& color_rt = *static_cast<const VulkanRenderTarget*>(
  3434:       depth_and_color_render_targets[color_index]);
  3435:   VkFormat color_format = GetColorVulkanFormat(color_rt.key().GetColorFormat());
  3436:   if (color_format == VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED) {
  3437:     return base;
  3438:   }
  3439: 
  3440:   const ui::vulkan::VulkanDevice* const vulkan_device =
  3441:       command_processor_.GetVulkanDevice();
  3442:   const ui::vulkan::VulkanDevice::Functions& dfn = vulkan_device->functions();
  3443:   const VkDevice device = vulkan_device->device();
  3444: 
  3445:   // Sample count must MATCH the LLE color image exactly (the seed/mirror
  3446:   // vkCmdCopyImage requires identical sample counts) - replicate CreateRenderTarget's
  3447:   // 2x->4x fallback.
  3448:   VkSampleCountFlagBits samples;
  3449:   if (render_pass_key.msaa_samples == xenos::MsaaSamples::k2X &&
  3450:       !msaa_2x_attachments_supported_) {
  3451:     samples = VK_SAMPLE_COUNT_4_BIT;
  3452:   } else {
  3453:     samples = VkSampleCountFlagBits(uint32_t(1)
  3454:                                     << uint32_t(render_pass_key.msaa_samples));
  3455:   }
  3456: 
  3457:   VkImageCreateInfo image_create_info;
  3458:   image_create_info.sType = VK_STRUCTURE_TYPE_IMAGE_CREATE_INFO;
  3459:   image_create_info.pNext = nullptr;
  3460:   image_create_info.flags = 0;
  3461:   image_create_info.imageType = VK_IMAGE_TYPE_2D;
  3462:   image_create_info.format = color_format;
  3463:   image_create_info.extent.width = base->host_extent.width;
  3464:   image_create_info.extent.height = base->host_extent.height;
  3465:   image_create_info.extent.depth = 1;
  3466:   image_create_info.mipLevels = 1;
  3467:   image_create_info.arrayLayers = 1;
  3468:   image_create_info.samples = samples;
  3469:   image_create_info.tiling = VK_IMAGE_TILING_OPTIMAL;
  3470:   image_create_info.usage = VK_IMAGE_USAGE_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_BIT |
  3471:                             VK_IMAGE_USAGE_TRANSFER_SRC_BIT |
  3472:                             VK_IMAGE_USAGE_TRANSFER_DST_BIT |
  3473:                             // LEVEL 5 (generation bridge): consumers (composite
  3474:                             // sampler / present) bind this image directly, so it
  3475:                             // must be sampleable.
  3476:                             VK_IMAGE_USAGE_SAMPLED_BIT;
  3477:   image_create_info.sharingMode = VK_SHARING_MODE_EXCLUSIVE;
  3478:   image_create_info.queueFamilyIndexCount = 0;
  3479:   image_create_info.pQueueFamilyIndices = nullptr;
  3480:   image_create_info.initialLayout = VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_UNDEFINED;
  3481:   VkImage native_image = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
  3482:   VkDeviceMemory native_memory = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
  3483:   if (!ui::vulkan::util::CreateDedicatedAllocationImage(
  3484:           vulkan_device, image_create_info,
  3485:           ui::vulkan::util::MemoryPurpose::kDeviceLocal, native_image,
  3486:           native_memory)) {
  3487:     XELOGE("BD L4: failed to create native color producer image");
  3488:     return base;
  3489:   }
  3490: 
  3491:   VkImageViewCreateInfo view_create_info;
  3492:   view_create_info.sType = VK_STRUCTURE_TYPE_IMAGE_VIEW_CREATE_INFO;
  3493:   view_create_info.pNext = nullptr;
  3494:   view_create_info.flags = 0;
  3495:   view_create_info.image = native_image;
  3496:   view_create_info.viewType = VK_IMAGE_VIEW_TYPE_2D;
  3497:   view_create_info.format = color_format;
  3498:   view_create_info.components.r = VK_COMPONENT_SWIZZLE_IDENTITY;
  3499:   view_create_info.components.g = VK_COMPONENT_SWIZZLE_IDENTITY;
  3500:   view_create_info.components.b = VK_COMPONENT_SWIZZLE_IDENTITY;
  3501:   view_create_info.components.a = VK_COMPONENT_SWIZZLE_IDENTITY;
  3502:   view_create_info.subresourceRange.aspectMask = VK_IMAGE_ASPECT_COLOR_BIT;
  3503:   view_create_info.subresourceRange.baseMipLevel = 0;
  3504:   view_create_info.subresourceRange.levelCount = 1;
  3505:   view_create_info.subresourceRange.baseArrayLayer = 0;
  3506:   view_create_info.subresourceRange.layerCount = 1;
  3507:   VkImageView native_view = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
  3508:   if (dfn.vkCreateImageView(device, &view_create_info, nullptr, &native_view) !=
  3509:       VK_SUCCESS) {
  3510:     XELOGE("BD L4: failed to create native color producer image view");
  3511:     dfn.vkDestroyImage(device, native_image, nullptr);
  3512:     dfn.vkFreeMemory(device, native_memory, nullptr);
  3513:     return base;
  3514:   }
  3515: 
  3516:   // Build the alternate framebuffer: same attachment ORDER as
  3517:   // GetHostRenderTargetsFramebuffer (depth first at bit 0 if present, then the
  3518:   // single color), but the color attachment view is the native image's view.
  3519:   VkRenderPass render_pass = GetHostRenderTargetsRenderPass(render_pass_key);
  3520:   VkImageView attachments[2];
  3521:   uint32_t attachment_count = 0;
  3522:   if (has_depth) {
  3523:     attachments[attachment_count++] = depth_rt->view_depth_stencil();
  3524:   }
  3525:   attachments[attachment_count++] = native_view;
  3526:   VkFramebufferCreateInfo framebuffer_create_info;
  3527:   framebuffer_create_info.sType = VK_STRUCTURE_TYPE_FRAMEBUFFER_CREATE_INFO;
  3528:   framebuffer_create_info.pNext = nullptr;
  3529:   framebuffer_create_info.flags = 0;
  3530:   framebuffer_create_info.renderPass = render_pass;
  3531:   framebuffer_create_info.attachmentCount = attachment_count;
  3532:   framebuffer_create_info.pAttachments = attachments;
  3533:   framebuffer_create_info.width = base->host_extent.width;
  3534:   framebuffer_create_info.height = base->host_extent.height;
  3535:   framebuffer_create_info.layers = 1;
  3536:   VkFramebuffer native_framebuffer = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
  3537:   if (render_pass == VK_NULL_HANDLE ||
  3538:       dfn.vkCreateFramebuffer(device, &framebuffer_create_info, nullptr,
  3539:                               &native_framebuffer) != VK_SUCCESS) {
  3540:     XELOGE("BD L4: failed to create native color producer framebuffer");
  3541:     dfn.vkDestroyImageView(device, native_view, nullptr);
  3542:     dfn.vkDestroyImage(device, native_image, nullptr);
  3543:     dfn.vkFreeMemory(device, native_memory, nullptr);
  3544:     return base;
  3545:   }
  3546: 
  3547:   // Cache on the base entry (const_cast: framebuffers_ owns a mutable entry; the
  3548:   // getter returns const for read-only key lookups, but this augments lifetime-
  3549:   // managed members). Safe: emplace guarantees a persistent address.
  3550:   Framebuffer& entry = const_cast<Framebuffer&>(*base);
  3551:   entry.bd_native_color_image = native_image;
  3552:   entry.bd_native_color_memory = native_memory;
  3553:   entry.bd_native_color_view = native_view;
  3554:   entry.bd_native_color_framebuffer = native_framebuffer;
  3555:   entry.bd_native_color_lle_image = color_rt.image();
  3556:   entry.bd_native_color_lle_rt =
  3557:       const_cast<RenderTarget*>(depth_and_color_render_targets[color_index]);
  3558:   entry.bd_native_color_format = color_format;
  3559:   XELOGI("BD L4: native color producer framebuffer created {}x{} fmt={} depth={}",
  3560:          base->host_extent.width, base->host_extent.height,
  3561:          uint32_t(color_rt.key().GetColorFormat()), has_depth ? 1 : 0);
  3562:   return base;
  3563: }

  3567:   if (!fb || fb->bd_native_color_image == VK_NULL_HANDLE ||
  3568:       fb->bd_native_color_format == VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED) {
  3569:     return VK_NULL_HANDLE;
  3570:   }
  3571:   Framebuffer& mfb = const_cast<Framebuffer&>(*fb);
  3572:   auto it = mfb.bd_native_color_swizzled_views_.find(host_swizzle);
  3573:   if (it != mfb.bd_native_color_swizzled_views_.end()) {
  3574:     return it->second;
  3575:   }
  3576:   const ui::vulkan::VulkanDevice* const vulkan_device =

  3565: VkImageView VulkanRenderTargetCache::GetBdNativeColorSwizzledView(
  3566:     const Framebuffer* fb, uint32_t host_swizzle) {
  3567:   if (!fb || fb->bd_native_color_image == VK_NULL_HANDLE ||
  3568:       fb->bd_native_color_format == VK_FORMAT_UNDEFINED) {
  3569:     return VK_NULL_HANDLE;

  3626: bool VulkanRenderTargetCache::SeedBdNativeColorProducer(const Framebuffer* fb) {
  3627:   if (!fb || fb->bd_native_color_image == VK_NULL_HANDLE ||
  3628:       !fb->bd_native_color_lle_rt) {
  3629:     return false;
  3630:   }
  3631:   VulkanRenderTarget& lle_rt =
  3632:       *static_cast<VulkanRenderTarget*>(fb->bd_native_color_lle_rt);
  3633:   Framebuffer& mfb = const_cast<Framebuffer&>(*fb);
  3634:   VkImageSubresourceRange range = {};
  3635:   range.aspectMask = VK_IMAGE_ASPECT_COLOR_BIT;
  3636:   range.levelCount = 1;
  3637:   range.layerCount = 1;
  3638:   // LLE color -> TRANSFER_SRC (tracked via SetUsage so downstream sees it). Left
  3639:   // in TRANSFER_SRC after the pass (5.6-sol: no fictional COLOR write-back; the
  3640:   // next real consumer transitions from the actual last usage).
  3641:   command_processor_.PushImageMemoryBarrier(
  3642:       lle_rt.image(), range, lle_rt.current_stage_mask(),
  3643:       VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_TRANSFER_BIT, lle_rt.current_access_mask(),
  3644:       VK_ACCESS_TRANSFER_READ_BIT, lle_rt.current_layout(),
  3645:       VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL);
  3646:   lle_rt.SetUsage(VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_TRANSFER_BIT, VK_ACCESS_TRANSFER_READ_BIT,
  3647:                   VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL);
  3648:   // Native color -> TRANSFER_DST. srcStage/srcAccess = the native image's REAL
  3649:   // prior state (the previous mirror's outstanding TRANSFER_READ, or TOP/0 on
  3650:   // first use) so the new transfer WRITE waits for that read = the WAR fix.
  3651:   // oldLayout=UNDEFINED still discards the (about-to-be-overwritten) contents.
  3652:   command_processor_.PushImageMemoryBarrier(
  3653:       fb->bd_native_color_image, range, fb->bd_native_color_stage,
  3654:       VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_TRANSFER_BIT, fb->bd_native_color_access,
  3655:       VK_ACCESS_TRANSFER_WRITE_BIT, VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_UNDEFINED,
  3656:       VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL);
  3657:   command_processor_.SubmitBarriers(true);
  3658:   VkImageCopy creg = {};
  3659:   creg.srcSubresource.aspectMask = VK_IMAGE_ASPECT_COLOR_BIT;
  3660:   creg.srcSubresource.layerCount = 1;
  3661:   creg.dstSubresource.aspectMask = VK_IMAGE_ASPECT_COLOR_BIT;
  3662:   creg.dstSubresource.layerCount = 1;
  3663:   creg.extent = {fb->host_extent.width, fb->host_extent.height, 1};
  3664:   command_processor_.deferred_command_buffer().CmdVkCopyImage(
  3665:       lle_rt.image(), VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL,
  3666:       fb->bd_native_color_image, VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL, 1, &creg);
  3667:   // Native -> COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL (the render pass's initial layout).
  3668:   command_processor_.PushImageMemoryBarrier(
  3669:       fb->bd_native_color_image, range, VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_TRANSFER_BIT,
  3670:       VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT_BIT, VK_ACCESS_TRANSFER_WRITE_BIT,
  3671:       VK_ACCESS_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_READ_BIT | VK_ACCESS_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_WRITE_BIT,
  3672:       VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL,
  3673:       VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL);
  3674:   command_processor_.SubmitBarriers(true);
  3675:   // The native image will next be written by the render pass as a color
  3676:   // attachment; the mirror records its post-pass state.
  3677:   mfb.bd_native_color_stage = VK_PIPELINE_STAGE_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OUTPUT_BIT;
  3678:   mfb.bd_native_color_access = VK_ACCESS_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_WRITE_BIT;
  3679:   mfb.bd_native_color_layout = VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL;
  3680:   return true;
  3681: }

  3683: void VulkanRenderTargetCache::MirrorBdNativeColorProducer(
  3684:     const Framebuffer* fb) {
  3685:   if (!fb || fb->bd_native_color_image == VK_NULL_HANDLE ||

  3695:   range.layerCount = 1;

  3697:   // TRANSFER_SRC.

  3704:   // the next real consumer transitions from there (no fictional COLOR write-back).
  3705:   command_processor_.PushImageMemoryBarrier(

  3717:   creg.dstSubresource.layerCount = 1;
  3718:   creg.extent = {fb->host_extent.width, fb->host_extent.height, 1};
  3719:   command_processor_.deferred_command_buffer().CmdVkCopyImage(
  3720:       fb->bd_native_color_image, VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL,
  3721:       lle_rt.image(), VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL, 1, &creg);
  3722:   command_processor_.SubmitBarriers(true);
  3723:   if (cvars::gpu_bd_native_color_lifetime_hle >= 5) {
  3724:     // LEVEL 5 generation bridge: also make the native image sampleable so the
  3725:     // composite sampler + present can read it directly (the CONSUME half). The
  3726:     // mirror above still keeps the LLE surface correct (un-dropped fallback), so
  3727:     // this stays correctness-neutral until transfers are actually dropped.
  3728:     command_processor_.PushImageMemoryBarrier(

  3732:         VK_IMAGE_LAYOUT_SHADER_READ_ONLY_OPTIMAL);

codex
The key simplification is emerging: if publication creates a logical texture image keyed by the resolve destination, that image is already a snapshot of guest resource state. Source-framebuffer reuse stops mattering. A per-generation pool is only mandatory if you keep aliasing the mutable producer image itself; I’m checking the resolve and texture-cache metadata now to pin down exactly where that snapshot should live and what must enter its key.

   712: VkImageView VulkanTextureCache::GetActiveBindingOrNullImageView(
   713:     uint32_t fetch_constant_index, xenos::FetchOpDimension dimension,
   714:     bool is_signed) const {
   715:   VkImageView image_view = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
   716:   const TextureBinding* binding = GetValidTextureBinding(fetch_constant_index);
   717:   if (binding && AreDimensionsCompatible(dimension, binding->key.dimension)) {
   718:     const VulkanTextureBinding& vulkan_binding =
   719:         vulkan_texture_bindings_[fetch_constant_index];
   720:     image_view = is_signed ? vulkan_binding.image_view_signed
   721:                            : vulkan_binding.image_view_unsigned;
   722:   }
   723:   if (image_view != VK_NULL_HANDLE) {
   724:     return image_view;
   725:   }
   726:   switch (dimension) {
   727:     case xenos::FetchOpDimension::k3DOrStacked:
   728:       return null_image_view_3d_;
   729:     case xenos::FetchOpDimension::kCube:
   730:       return null_image_view_cube_;
   731:     default:
   732:       return null_image_view_2d_array_;
   733:   }

codex
Use option **(b)** now, implemented as a destination-owned “native resolved texture,” not as another alias to the mutable framebuffer image. This fixes both extent and reuse. Option **(a)** is the later zero-copy optimization; **(c)** should be avoided.

### 1. Extent: publish an exact logical-size image

Keep the tile-rounded native producer RT for now. At the resolve/publication point, create or reuse a single-sample `VkImage` whose extent exactly matches the guest texture’s logical extent, then copy only the valid resolve rectangle:

```text
mutable producer RT: 720x1824
        copy region: 720x720
destination texture: 720x720
```

Use:

- `vkCmdCopyImage` for a same-format, single-sample, 1:1 resolve.
- `vkCmdResolveImage`, preferably an in-pass resolve attachment eventually, for MSAA → 1x.
- The existing resolve-conversion shader writing a storage image when Xenos format conversion is required.
- `vkCmdBlitImage` only for genuine scaling. Do not blit 720×1824 down to 720×720—that would squash the empty tail into the result.

Take `srcOffset`, `dstOffset`, and copied extent from `ResolveInfo`; don’t assume top-left except behind a BD-verified fast-path gate. The best hook is inside [`VulkanRenderTargetCache::Resolve`](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/vulkan/vulkan_render_target_cache.cc:1497>), where all resolve geometry and conversion semantics still exist, rather than the later `IssueCopy` publication code.

The published image should be keyed by the full logical texture identity:

```cpp
{base_page, mip_page, width, height, pitch, format,
 dimension, array/slice, tiled, mip_level}
```

The existing [`TextureKey`](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/texture_cache.h:125>) already contains most of this. Base address alone is only a BD-specific shortcut. Swizzle belongs to cached image views, not the storage key.

Option (a) is Vulkan-legal: the framebuffer may be 720×720 while the reused LLE depth attachment is taller, because attachments only need to be at least as large as the framebuffer. [The framebuffer validity rules explicitly permit this.](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkFramebufferCreateInfo.html) But it is correct only after proving:

- Every producer viewport/scissor write lies inside the logical extent.
- The resolve source and destination rectangles are 1:1.
- There is no conversion that direct rendering bypasses.
- No other resolve needs rows outside that logical rectangle.
- The producer generation can be associated with the destination before rendering.

So: **(b) first; promote verified identity resolves to (a) later.** UV scaling is brittle because it also affects derivatives/LOD, address-mode boundaries, offsets/gathers, and texture-size queries.

### 2. Lifetime: destination-owned snapshots eliminate the generation pool

Your current “generation” is metadata only. [`FinalizeBdNativeColorMirrorAfterPass`](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/vulkan/vulkan_command_processor.cc:5025>) increments a generation but stores the same framebuffer-owned `VkImageView`; it does not freeze those pixels.

With the logical publish image, the minimal design is:

```cpp
struct NativeResolvedTexture {
  NativeTextureKey key;
  VkImage image;
  VkImageView identity_view;
  unordered_map<uint32_t, VkImageView> swizzled_views;
  VkImageLayout layout;
  VkPipelineStageFlags stage;
  VkAccessFlags access;
  uint64_t content_serial;
  uint32_t last_publish_epoch;
};
```

Maintain one persistent `NativeResolvedTexture` per resolve-destination resource:

1. Field renders into mutable producer `P`.
2. Field resolve copies `P` into logical resource `T_field`.
3. `P` may immediately be reused for bloom or another pass.
4. Bloom resolve copies it into `T_bloom`.
5. Composite samples `T_field` and `T_bloom`, never `P`.

No per-generation allocation is needed for ordinary overwrites of the same destination. A later resolve to that same guest texture overwrites the same native image, matching guest-memory semantics. Explicit barriers order:

```text
previous shader read → transfer write
transfer write       → fragment shader read
```

That remains correct across frames on the same Vulkan queue. Track the real stage/access/layout, as Vulkan requires layout transitions and accesses to be properly ordered. [Vulkan synchronization rules](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/synchronization.html)

You only need submission-retired storage when:

- The resource’s extent/format changes and its old image must be replaced.
- Multiple queues access it concurrently.
- You retain the zero-copy producer-alias design.

For zero-copy producer aliasing, the minimal pool is rename-on-write:

- Re-begins before the resolve stay in the same active generation.
- Resolve publishes and freezes the active generation.
- The first subsequent write to that source framebuffer rotates to a new image.
- The frozen generation is recycled after the last referencing submission completes.
- Never recycle merely because the destination alias was replaced.

That is higher complexity than copy-on-publish and still requires solving the logical extent.

### 3. Is sampled-native worthwhile?

Yes—but formulate it as a **native resolve-texture cache**, not “bind the producer framebuffer directly.”

The clean architecture is for `VulkanTextureCache::GetActiveBindingOrNullImageView` to return the published native resolved texture when its full `TextureKey` matches. Then the composite shader and normal descriptor path remain unchanged. The current [`rt_as_texture_views_pixel_` redirect](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/vulkan/vulkan_command_processor.cc:11295>) is fine as a BD prototype, but it should bind the destination-owned image and create swizzled views from that image—not from the framebuffer.

If “keep composite LLE-sampled” means it still samples the shared-memory/EDRAM-derived texture, then no: the resolve, guest-memory write, invalidation/upload, and ownership path remain load-bearing. If it means leaving the normal texture-cache binding code intact while that cache supplies a native resolved image, then yes—that is precisely the recommended version.

On Adreno, copy-on-publish is a correctness bridge, not necessarily the final fastest path. Sampling an intermediate in a later, filtered pass generally requires materializing it outside tile memory; an explicit copy may add another round-trip. Vulkan’s own TBR guidance warns that separate blits/resolves can force external-memory traffic. [Vulkan TBR guidance](https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/tile_based_rendering_best_practices.html)

The progression should therefore be:

1. Destination-owned logical images via copy-on-resolve; prove perfect pixels.
2. Drop only the EDRAM resolve/ownership operations whose native publication succeeded.
3. Measure on Thor.
4. Promote hot, full, 1:1 field/bloom resolves to direct logical rendering or an in-pass MSAA resolve.
5. Keep copy/conversion publication as the correctness fallback.

The target is realistically **one unavoidable native materialization per sampled intermediate, zero emulated EDRAM ownership round-trips**. Pure GMEM retention would require producer/composite pass fusion or tile-local sampling, which is substantially more invasive and generally unsuitable for bloom’s filtered, cross-pixel reads.
tokens used
184,064
Use option **(b)** now, implemented as a destination-owned “native resolved texture,” not as another alias to the mutable framebuffer image. This fixes both extent and reuse. Option **(a)** is the later zero-copy optimization; **(c)** should be avoided.

### 1. Extent: publish an exact logical-size image

Keep the tile-rounded native producer RT for now. At the resolve/publication point, create or reuse a single-sample `VkImage` whose extent exactly matches the guest texture’s logical extent, then copy only the valid resolve rectangle:

```text
mutable producer RT: 720x1824
        copy region: 720x720
destination texture: 720x720
```

Use:

- `vkCmdCopyImage` for a same-format, single-sample, 1:1 resolve.
- `vkCmdResolveImage`, preferably an in-pass resolve attachment eventually, for MSAA → 1x.
- The existing resolve-conversion shader writing a storage image when Xenos format conversion is required.
- `vkCmdBlitImage` only for genuine scaling. Do not blit 720×1824 down to 720×720—that would squash the empty tail into the result.

Take `srcOffset`, `dstOffset`, and copied extent from `ResolveInfo`; don’t assume top-left except behind a BD-verified fast-path gate. The best hook is inside [`VulkanRenderTargetCache::Resolve`](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/vulkan/vulkan_render_target_cache.cc:1497>), where all resolve geometry and conversion semantics still exist, rather than the later `IssueCopy` publication code.

The published image should be keyed by the full logical texture identity:

```cpp
{base_page, mip_page, width, height, pitch, format,
 dimension, array/slice, tiled, mip_level}
```

The existing [`TextureKey`](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/texture_cache.h:125>) already contains most of this. Base address alone is only a BD-specific shortcut. Swizzle belongs to cached image views, not the storage key.

Option (a) is Vulkan-legal: the framebuffer may be 720×720 while the reused LLE depth attachment is taller, because attachments only need to be at least as large as the framebuffer. [The framebuffer validity rules explicitly permit this.](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkFramebufferCreateInfo.html) But it is correct only after proving:

- Every producer viewport/scissor write lies inside the logical extent.
- The resolve source and destination rectangles are 1:1.
- There is no conversion that direct rendering bypasses.
- No other resolve needs rows outside that logical rectangle.
- The producer generation can be associated with the destination before rendering.

So: **(b) first; promote verified identity resolves to (a) later.** UV scaling is brittle because it also affects derivatives/LOD, address-mode boundaries, offsets/gathers, and texture-size queries.

### 2. Lifetime: destination-owned snapshots eliminate the generation pool

Your current “generation” is metadata only. [`FinalizeBdNativeColorMirrorAfterPass`](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/vulkan/vulkan_command_processor.cc:5025>) increments a generation but stores the same framebuffer-owned `VkImageView`; it does not freeze those pixels.

With the logical publish image, the minimal design is:

```cpp
struct NativeResolvedTexture {
  NativeTextureKey key;
  VkImage image;
  VkImageView identity_view;
  unordered_map<uint32_t, VkImageView> swizzled_views;
  VkImageLayout layout;
  VkPipelineStageFlags stage;
  VkAccessFlags access;
  uint64_t content_serial;
  uint32_t last_publish_epoch;
};
```

Maintain one persistent `NativeResolvedTexture` per resolve-destination resource:

1. Field renders into mutable producer `P`.
2. Field resolve copies `P` into logical resource `T_field`.
3. `P` may immediately be reused for bloom or another pass.
4. Bloom resolve copies it into `T_bloom`.
5. Composite samples `T_field` and `T_bloom`, never `P`.

No per-generation allocation is needed for ordinary overwrites of the same destination. A later resolve to that same guest texture overwrites the same native image, matching guest-memory semantics. Explicit barriers order:

```text
previous shader read → transfer write
transfer write       → fragment shader read
```

That remains correct across frames on the same Vulkan queue. Track the real stage/access/layout, as Vulkan requires layout transitions and accesses to be properly ordered. [Vulkan synchronization rules](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/synchronization.html)

You only need submission-retired storage when:

- The resource’s extent/format changes and its old image must be replaced.
- Multiple queues access it concurrently.
- You retain the zero-copy producer-alias design.

For zero-copy producer aliasing, the minimal pool is rename-on-write:

- Re-begins before the resolve stay in the same active generation.
- Resolve publishes and freezes the active generation.
- The first subsequent write to that source framebuffer rotates to a new image.
- The frozen generation is recycled after the last referencing submission completes.
- Never recycle merely because the destination alias was replaced.

That is higher complexity than copy-on-publish and still requires solving the logical extent.

### 3. Is sampled-native worthwhile?

Yes—but formulate it as a **native resolve-texture cache**, not “bind the producer framebuffer directly.”

The clean architecture is for `VulkanTextureCache::GetActiveBindingOrNullImageView` to return the published native resolved texture when its full `TextureKey` matches. Then the composite shader and normal descriptor path remain unchanged. The current [`rt_as_texture_views_pixel_` redirect](<C:/Users/leanerdesigner/Documents/New project 8/xenia-thor/src/xenia/gpu/vulkan/vulkan_command_processor.cc:11295>) is fine as a BD prototype, but it should bind the destination-owned image and create swizzled views from that image—not from the framebuffer.

If “keep composite LLE-sampled” means it still samples the shared-memory/EDRAM-derived texture, then no: the resolve, guest-memory write, invalidation/upload, and ownership path remain load-bearing. If it means leaving the normal texture-cache binding code intact while that cache supplies a native resolved image, then yes—that is precisely the recommended version.

On Adreno, copy-on-publish is a correctness bridge, not necessarily the final fastest path. Sampling an intermediate in a later, filtered pass generally requires materializing it outside tile memory; an explicit copy may add another round-trip. Vulkan’s own TBR guidance warns that separate blits/resolves can force external-memory traffic. [Vulkan TBR guidance](https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/tile_based_rendering_best_practices.html)

The progression should therefore be:

1. Destination-owned logical images via copy-on-resolve; prove perfect pixels.
2. Drop only the EDRAM resolve/ownership operations whose native publication succeeded.
3. Measure on Thor.
4. Promote hot, full, 1:1 field/bloom resolves to direct logical rendering or an in-pass MSAA resolve.
5. Keep copy/conversion publication as the correctness fallback.

The target is realistically **one unavoidable native materialization per sampled intermediate, zero emulated EDRAM ownership round-trips**. Pure GMEM retention would require producer/composite pass fusion or tile-local sampling, which is substantially more invasive and generally unsuitable for bloom’s filtered, cross-pixel reads.
